    assert text.index("## Deliverable") < text.index("## Context")


def test_derive_tree_imports_disposable_clone_objects(tmp_path, monkeypatch):
    root = repo(tmp_path)
    candidate = git(root, "rev-parse", "HEAD")

    def project(scratch, turn, cand, tier="all", primary_root=None):
        (Path(scratch) / "derived").write_text("exact\n")
        git(scratch, "add", "-A")
        return ["WI-1"], {"outcome": "return"}, None

    monkeypatch.setattr(pp, "_project", project)
    tree, closed, plan, refusal = pp.derive_tree(root, {}, candidate)
    assert refusal is None and closed == ["WI-1"]
    assert git(root, "cat-file", "-p", tree)


def test_project_runs_candidate_bar_with_primary_harness_python(tmp_path, monkeypatch):
    """Product effects run candidate check.py through the primary checkout venv."""
    primary = tmp_path / "primary"
    scratch = tmp_path / "scratch"
    primary.mkdir()
    scratch.mkdir()
    for root in (primary, scratch):
        git(root, "init", "-q")
        git(root, "config", "user.name", "Test")
        git(root, "config", "user.email", "test@example.invalid")
    sentinel = primary / "harness-used"
    py = primary / ".venv/bin/python"
    py.parent.mkdir(parents=True)
    py.write_text(
        '#!/bin/sh\nprintf used > {!r}\nexec {!r} "$@"\n'.format(
            str(sentinel), sys.executable
        ),
        encoding="utf-8",
    )
    py.chmod(0o755)
    scripts = scratch / "scripts"
    scripts.mkdir()
    (scripts / "trunk_step.py").write_text("pass\n", encoding="utf-8")
    (scripts / "check.py").write_text(
        "import pathlib\n"
        "assert pathlib.Path('candidate-only.txt').is_file()\n"
        "print('  PASS  candidate-check   0.0s')\n",
        encoding="utf-8",
    )
    (scratch / "candidate-only.txt").write_text("product effect\n", encoding="utf-8")
    git(scratch, "add", "-A")
    git(scratch, "commit", "-qm", "candidate")
    candidate = git(scratch, "rev-parse", "HEAD")

    monkeypatch.setattr(pp.primary_turn, "validate_candidate", lambda *_a: None)
    monkeypatch.setattr(
        pp.primary_approval,
        "approval_projection",
        lambda *_a, **_k: (
            {
                "outcome": "return",
                "statuses": {},
                "approves": {},
                "author": None,
                "findings": [],
                "judgment": "docs/reviews/judgment.md",
            },
            None,
        ),
    )
    monkeypatch.setattr(pp, "_apply_approval", lambda *_a: None)

    def stage_product(root, *_a, **_k):
        (Path(root) / "candidate-only.txt").write_text(
            "published product effect\n", encoding="utf-8"
        )
        git(root, "add", "candidate-only.txt")
        return ["WI-1"], [], None

    monkeypatch.setattr(pp.primary_close, "stage_primary_adjudication", stage_product)
    monkeypatch.setattr(pp.intake, "stage_mint", lambda *_a, **_k: ([], None))

    closed, plan, refusal = pp._project(
        scratch,
        {"wi": "WI-1", "spec": "docs/work/queued/WI-1.md"},
        candidate,
        "smoke",
        primary,
    )

    assert refusal is None and closed == ["WI-1"]
    assert plan["verification"]["mode"] == "product-bar"
    assert sentinel.read_text(encoding="utf-8") == "used"


@pytest.mark.parametrize("product_bar", [False, True], ids=["scoped", "required-bar"])
def test_real_return_publish_and_completion_replay(tmp_path, monkeypatch, product_bar):
    root = repo(tmp_path)
    spec = root / "docs/work/queued/WI-1-x.md"
    spec.write_text(
        '+++\nid = "WI-1"\ntitle = "clean close"\nsafety_class = "adjudication"\nspecref = "x"\n+++\n\n## Context\n\njudge\n'
    )
    step = root / "project-trajectory/scripts/trunk_step.py"
    step.parent.mkdir(parents=True)
    step.write_text("import sys\nsys.exit(0)\n")
    if product_bar:
        step.write_text(
            "from pathlib import Path\n"
            "Path('derived-product').write_text('regenerated\\n')\n"
        )
        (step.parent / "check.py").write_text(
            "from pathlib import Path\n"
            "assert Path('derived-product').read_text() == 'regenerated\\n'\n"
            "Path('bar-ran').write_text('checked\\n')\n"
            "print('  PASS  candidate-check   0.0s')\n"
        )
    git(root, "add", "-A")
    git(root, "commit", "-qm", "base")
    turn = primary_adjudication.begin(root, "WI-1")
    base = turn["base"]
    turn_id = turn["turn"]
    proposal = verdict.tree_identity(root, base)
    approval = (
        '\n## Approval\n\n```toml\nversion = 1\nturn = "{}"\n'
        'base = "{}"\nproposal = "{}"\ndecision = []\n```\n'
    ).format(turn_id, base, proposal)
    spec.write_text(spec.read_text() + approval)
    log = root / "docs/iteration/WI-1.log"
    log.parent.mkdir(parents=True)
    log.write_text(
        "# phase: ADJUDICATE\n# wi: WI-1\n# base: {}\n# provider: test\n# roster-row: test\n# model: test\n# session-id: s\n# invocation-id: i\n".format(
            base[:12]
        )
    )
    review = root / "docs/reviews/WI-1-001-REVIEW-A.md"
    review.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    review.write_text("VERDICT: APPROVE\nOUTCOME: REVIEWED\n")
    git(root, "add", "-A")
    git(root, "commit", "-qm", "ADJUDICATE verdict")
    candidate = git(root, "rev-parse", "HEAD")
    log.write_text(
        log.read_text()
        + "# primary-turn: {}\n# judgment: docs/reviews/WI-1-001-REVIEW-A.md\n# candidate: {}\n".format(
            turn_id, candidate
        )
    )
    git(root, "add", "-A")
    git(root, "commit", "-qm", "ADJUDICATE telemetry")
    candidate = git(root, "rev-parse", "HEAD")
    hook = root / ".git/hooks/pre-commit"
    hook.write_text("#!/bin/sh\nexit 1\n")
    hook.chmod(0o755)
    failed, refusal = pp.publish(root, turn, candidate)
    assert failed is None and "commit failed" in refusal
    assert git(root, "rev-parse", "HEAD") == candidate
    assert git(root, "status", "--porcelain") == ""
    hook.unlink()
    hook.write_text(
        "#!/bin/sh\nprintf 'smuggled\\n' > hook-smuggled\ngit add hook-smuggled\n",
        encoding="utf-8",
    )
    hook.chmod(0o755)
    closed, refusal = pp.publish(root, turn, candidate)
    assert closed is None
    assert refusal == "publication commit tree differs from the verified tree"
    assert git(root, "rev-parse", "HEAD") == candidate
    assert git(root, "status", "--porcelain") == ""
    assert not (root / "hook-smuggled").exists()
    assert not pp.completed(root, turn)
    hook.unlink()
    closed, refusal = pp.publish(root, turn, candidate)
    assert refusal is None and closed == ["WI-1"]
    assert (root / "docs/archive/work/complete/WI-1-x.md").is_file()
    landed = git(root, "rev-parse", "HEAD")
    assert pp.completed(root, turn) and pp.publication_commit(root, turn) == landed
    assert pp.completed(root, turn)  # exact replay is idempotent; no new commit
    assert git(root, "rev-parse", "HEAD") == landed
    # Both readers use the same verified carrier; the reset reader must not.
    identity = verdict.governing_identity(
        root, turn["primary_branch"], candidate, carrier=pp.carrier_candidate
    )
    # A cold query must preserve dirty as well as clean primary state. Compare
    # every file, including index, refs, worktree, objects and FETCH_HEAD.
    if product_bar:
        assert (root / "bar-ran").read_text() == "checked\n"
    (root / "staged-sentinel").write_text("staged\n")
    git(root, "add", "staged-sentinel")
    (root / "staged-sentinel").write_text("unstaged over staged\n")
    (root / "untracked-sentinel").write_text("untracked\n")
    before = {
        p.relative_to(root): p.read_bytes() for p in root.rglob("*") if p.is_file()
    }
    pp._VERIFIED_PUBLICATIONS.clear()
    assert pp.carrier_candidate(root, landed) == candidate
    after = {
        p.relative_to(root): p.read_bytes() for p in root.rglob("*") if p.is_file()
    }
    assert after == before
    git(root, "reset", "--hard", landed)
    (root / "untracked-sentinel").unlink()
    assert (
        verdict.governing_identity(
            root, turn["primary_branch"], carrier=pp.carrier_candidate
        )
        == identity
    )
    assert verdict.work_tip(root, turn["primary_branch"]) == landed
    message = git(root, "show", "-s", "--format=%B", landed)
    final_tree = git(root, "rev-parse", landed + "^{tree}")
    candidate_tree = git(root, "rev-parse", candidate + "^{tree}")
    for forged_message, forged_tree, parent in (
        (message, candidate_tree, candidate),  # missing the derived effects
        (message, final_tree, landed),  # copied true words onto later work
        (message.replace(turn_id, "WI-999-" + base), final_tree, candidate),
        (message.replace(candidate, base), final_tree, candidate),
        (
            message.replace('"result": "pass"', '"result": "fail"'),
            final_tree,
            candidate,
        ),
        (message + "\n" + pp.TRAILER + turn_id, final_tree, candidate),
    ):
        forged = git(
            root, "commit-tree", forged_tree, "-p", parent, "-m", forged_message
        )
        assert pp.carrier_candidate(root, forged) is None
        if forged_message == message + "\n" + pp.TRAILER + turn_id:
            assert pp._verified_candidate(root, turn, forged) is None
    bad_author = subprocess.run(
        [
            "git",
            "-C",
            str(root),
            "commit-tree",
            final_tree,
            "-p",
            candidate,
            "-m",
            message,
        ],
        env=dict(
            os.environ,
            GIT_AUTHOR_NAME="Wrong author",
            GIT_AUTHOR_EMAIL="wrong@example.invalid",
        ),
        capture_output=True,
        text=True,
        check=True,
    ).stdout.strip()
    assert pp.carrier_candidate(root, bad_author) is None
    original_derive = pp.derive_tree
    calls = []

    def counted_derive(*args, **kwargs):
        calls.append(args[2])
        return original_derive(*args, **kwargs)

    monkeypatch.setattr(pp, "derive_tree", counted_derive)
    assert pp.completed(root, turn)
    assert pp.pending(root) is None
    assert calls == []
    mismatched_turn = dict(turn, primary_root="wrong-primary-root")
    assert not pp.completed(root, mismatched_turn)
    assert calls == [candidate]
    calls.clear()
    forged_tree = git(root, "show", "-s", "--format=%T", "HEAD")
    git(
        root,
        "commit",
        "--allow-empty",
        "-qm",
        "forged publication\n\n{}{}\nPublication-Candidate: {}\n"
        "Publication-Tree: {}".format(pp.TRAILER, turn["turn"], landed, forged_tree),
    )
    assert pp.completed(root, turn)
    assert calls == [landed]


def test_bootstrapped_return_publication_replay_keeps_real_regeneration_fresh(
    tmp_path,
):
    root = tmp_path / "scaffold"
    root.mkdir()
    boot = run_py([SCRIPTS / "bootstrap.py", "--dest", root], cwd=tmp_path)
    assert boot.returncode == 0, boot.stdout + boot.stderr
    make_minimal_project(root)
    set_process_key(root, "policies", "review_rounds", 0)
    spec = root / "docs/work/queued/WI-1-return.md"
    spec.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    spec.write_text(
        '+++\nid = "WI-1"\ntitle = "record a finite return"\n'
        'safety_class = "adjudication"\nspecref = "docs/log.md"\n+++\n'
        "\n## Context\n\nJudge the empty scope.\n",
        encoding="utf-8",
    )
    record_ids(root)
    git(root, "init", "-q")
    git(root, "config", "user.name", "Test")
    git(root, "config", "user.email", "test@example.invalid")
    git(root, "add", "-A")
    git(root, "commit", "-qm", "seed bootstrapped primary")
    turn = primary_adjudication.begin(root, "WI-1")
    base = turn["base"]
    verdict_path = root / "docs/reviews/WI-1-primary.md"
    verdict_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    verdict_path.write_text("OUTCOME: RETURN\n", encoding="utf-8")
    git(root, "add", "-A")
    git(root, "commit", "-qm", "typed primary return")
    spec.write_text(
        spec.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
        + '\n## Approval\n\n```toml\nversion = 1\nturn = "{}"\n'
        'base = "{}"\nproposal = "{}"\ndecision = []\n```\n'.format(
            turn["turn"], base, verdict.tree_identity(root, base)
        ),
        encoding="utf-8",
    )
    git(root, "add", "-A")
    git(root, "commit", "-qm", "record primary return payload")
    reviewed = git(root, "rev-parse", "HEAD")
    telemetry = root / "docs/iteration/WI-1-primary.log"
    telemetry.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    telemetry.write_text(
        "# phase: ADJUDICATE\n# wi: WI-1\n# provider: test\n"
        "# roster-row: route\n# model: model\n# session-id: session\n"
        "# invocation-id: invocation\n# base: {}\n# primary-turn: {}\n"
        "# judgment: docs/reviews/WI-1-primary.md\n# candidate: {}\n# ---\n".format(
            base[:12], turn["turn"], reviewed
        ),
        encoding="utf-8",
    )
    git(root, "add", "-A")
    git(root, "commit", "-qm", "bind primary return telemetry")
    candidate = git(root, "rev-parse", "HEAD")
    linked = tmp_path / "linked-worker"
    git(root, "worktree", "add", "--detach", str(linked), candidate)
    primary_head = git(root, "rev-parse", "HEAD")
    linked_head = git(linked, "rev-parse", "HEAD")

    closed, refusal = pp.publish(linked, turn, candidate)

    assert closed is None
    assert refusal == "adjudication needs the designated primary checkout"
    assert git(root, "rev-parse", "HEAD") == primary_head
    assert git(linked, "rev-parse", "HEAD") == linked_head
    assert git(root, "status", "--porcelain") == ""
    assert git(linked, "status", "--porcelain") == ""

    closed, refusal = pp.publish(root, turn, candidate)

    assert refusal is None and closed == ["WI-1"]
    landed = git(root, "rev-parse", "HEAD")
    assert pp.completed(root, turn)
    assert git(root, "rev-parse", "HEAD") == landed
    for script in ("derive_stage.py", "gen_trajectory.py"):
        check = subprocess.run(
            [sys.executable, "scripts/" + script, "--check"],
            cwd=root,
            capture_output=True,
            text=True,
        )
        assert check.returncode == 0, check.stdout + check.stderr
    assert git(root, "status", "--porcelain") == ""
    before = {
        path: path.read_bytes()
        for path in root.rglob("*")
        if path.is_file() and ".git" not in path.parts
    }
    regen = subprocess.run(
        [sys.executable, "scripts/trunk_step.py", "--root", ".", "--regen"],
        cwd=root,
        capture_output=True,
        text=True,
    )
    assert regen.returncode == 0, regen.stdout + regen.stderr
    after = {path: path.read_bytes() for path in before}
    dashboard = root / "PROJECT_STATE.html"
    stage = root / "docs/stage"
    # Both writers declare their revision/date comments informational: their
    # --check paths compare dashboard content without ASOF_RE and stage through
    # field_block, because a commit necessarily changes its own as-of identity.
    assert {path for path in before if before[path] != after[path]} == {
        dashboard,
        stage,
    }
    assert gen_trajectory.ASOF_RE.sub("", after[dashboard].decode()) == (
        gen_trajectory.ASOF_RE.sub("", before[dashboard].decode())
    )
    assert kitstage.field_block(kitstage.parse(after[stage].decode())) == (
        kitstage.field_block(kitstage.parse(before[stage].decode()))
    )
    assert pp.completed(root, turn)
    git(root, "checkout", "--", "PROJECT_STATE.html", "docs/stage")
    assert git(root, "status", "--porcelain") == ""


def _first_approval_candidate(tmp_path, status_key="status"):
    root = repo(tmp_path)
    registry = root / "docs/requirements/low-level-requirements.toml"
    registry.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    registry.write_text(
        '[design.LLR-001]\ntitle="row"\ndetail="detail"\nrationale="why"\nstatus="Drafted"\n'.replace(
            "status=", status_key + "="
        )
    )
    snapshot = (
        root
        / "docs/archive/last_approved/docs/requirements/low-level-requirements.toml"
    )
    snapshot.parent.mkdir(parents=True)
    snapshot.write_bytes(registry.read_bytes())
    (root / "docs/process.toml").write_text(
        '[attestation]\nhuman_approval_through="DevStg-Below"\n'
    )
    (root / "docs/id-watermark").write_text("WI = 1\n")
    step = root / "project-trajectory/scripts/trunk_step.py"
    step.parent.mkdir(parents=True)
    step.write_text("import sys\nsys.exit(0)\n")
    spec = root / "docs/work/queued/WI-1-x.md"
    spec.write_text(
        '+++\nid="WI-1"\ntitle="approve"\nsafety_class="adjudication"\nbrief="first-approval"\nspecref="docs/requirements/low-level-requirements.toml"\nadjudicates=["LLR-001"]\n+++\n\n## Context\n\njudge\n'
    )
    git(root, "add", "-A")
    git(root, "commit", "-qm", "base")
    turn = primary_adjudication.begin(root, "WI-1")
    base = turn["base"]
    turn_id = turn["turn"]
    blob = git(
        root, "rev-parse", base + ":docs/requirements/low-level-requirements.toml"
    )
    proposal = verdict.tree_identity(root, base)
    evidence_file = root / "docs/reviews/judgment.md"
    evidence_file.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    evidence_file.write_text("OUTCOME: APPROVE rows=1\n")
    git(root, "add", "-A")
    git(root, "commit", "-qm", "judgment")
    evidence = git(root, "rev-parse", "HEAD")
    log = root / "docs/iteration/WI-1.log"
    log.parent.mkdir(parents=True)
    log.write_text(
        "# phase: ADJUDICATE\n# wi: WI-1\n# base: {}\n# provider: test\n# roster-row: route\n# model: model\n# session-id: s\n# invocation-id: i\n".format(
            base[:12]
        )
    )
    payload = """\n## Approval\n\n```toml\nversion=1\nturn="{}"\nbase="{}"\nproposal="{}"\n[[decision]]\nregistry="docs/requirements/low-level-requirements.toml"\nrow="LLR-001"\nclassification="approve"\nbefore={{Detail="{}"}}\nafter={{Detail="{}"}}\nevidence=["{}"]\n```\n\n## Dispositions\n\n```toml\ntitle="continue exact work"\nworkstream="process"\nneeds=["WI-1"]\n```\n""".format(
        turn_id, base, proposal, blob, blob, evidence
    )
    spec.write_text(spec.read_text() + payload)
    git(root, "add", "-A")
    git(root, "commit", "-qm", "ADJUDICATE result")
    judged_candidate = git(root, "rev-parse", "HEAD")
    log.write_text(
        log.read_text()
        + "# primary-turn: {}\n# judgment: docs/reviews/judgment.md\n# candidate: {}\n".format(
            turn_id, judged_candidate
        )
    )
    git(root, "add", "-A")
    git(root, "commit", "-qm", "ADJUDICATE telemetry")
    candidate = git(root, "rev-parse", "HEAD")
    return root, turn, candidate, registry, snapshot


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "carrier_fault",
    [
        "extra-path",
        "missing-path",
        "cross-WI-close",
        "malformed-successor",
        "partial-snapshot",
        "unreviewed-decision",
        "stale-proposal",
        "wrong-WI",
    ],
)
def test_real_first_approval_snapshot_and_successor_are_one_commit(
    tmp_path, monkeypatch, carrier_fault
):
    root, turn, candidate, registry, snapshot = _first_approval_candidate(tmp_path)
    turn_id = turn["turn"]
    monkeypatch.setattr(pp.baseline_snapshot, "_today", lambda: "2001-01-01")
    closed, refusal = pp.publish(root, turn, candidate)
    assert refusal is None and closed == ["WI-1"]
    assert 'status = "Approved"' in registry.read_text()
    assert snapshot.read_bytes() == registry.read_bytes()
    successors = list((root / "docs/work/queued").glob("WI-002-*.md"))
    assert len(successors) == 1 and 'needs = ["WI-1"]' in successors[0].read_text()
    assert "WI = 2" in (root / "docs/id-watermark").read_text()
    monkeypatch.setattr(pp.baseline_snapshot, "_today", lambda: "2099-12-31")
    assert pp.completed(root, turn)  # replay is independent of today's date
    assert git(root, "rev-list", "--count", candidate + "..HEAD") == "1"

    # Forge one defect onto a real accepted carrier. Keep its real candidate,
    # receipt and adjudicator author, so another invalid field cannot mask it.
    landed = git(root, "rev-parse", "HEAD")
    message = git(root, "show", "-s", "--format=%B", landed)
    author = git(root, "show", "-s", "--format=%an%x00%ae", landed).split("\0")
    complete = root / "docs/archive/work/complete/WI-1-x.md"
    selection = root / pp.primary_turn.record_path(turn)
    if carrier_fault == "extra-path":
        (root / "unexpected").write_text("unreviewed effect\n")
    elif carrier_fault == "missing-path":
        complete.unlink()
    elif carrier_fault == "cross-WI-close":
        complete.rename(complete.with_name("WI-999-x.md"))
    elif carrier_fault == "malformed-successor":
        successors[0].write_text("not a valid spec\n")
    elif carrier_fault == "partial-snapshot":
        snapshot.write_text(
            git(root, "show", candidate + ":" + snapshot.relative_to(root).as_posix())
        )
    elif carrier_fault == "unreviewed-decision":
        text = complete.read_text()
        assert 'classification="approve"' in text
        complete.write_text(
            text.replace('classification="approve"', 'classification="return"')
        )
    elif carrier_fault == "stale-proposal":
        text = selection.read_text()
        assert turn["proposal_spec_blob"] in text
        selection.write_text(text.replace(turn["proposal_spec_blob"], "0" * 40))
    else:
        assert carrier_fault == "wrong-WI"
        foreign = (
            selection.parent.with_name(
                selection.parent.name.replace("WI-1-", "WI-999-")
            )
            / selection.name
        )
        foreign.parent.mkdir()
        foreign.write_text(selection.read_text().replace("WI-1", "WI-999"))
        selection.unlink()
        message = message.replace(turn_id, turn_id.replace("WI-1-", "WI-999-"))
    git(root, "add", "-A")
    forged_tree = git(root, "write-tree")
    assert forged_tree != git(root, "rev-parse", landed + "^{tree}")
    forged = subprocess.check_output(
        [
            "git",
            "-C",
            str(root),
            "commit-tree",
            forged_tree,
            "-p",
            candidate,
            "-m",
            message,
        ],
        text=True,
        env=dict(os.environ, GIT_AUTHOR_NAME=author[0], GIT_AUTHOR_EMAIL=author[1]),
    ).strip()
    git(root, "reset", "--hard", landed)
    pp._VERIFIED_PUBLICATIONS.clear()
    assert pp.carrier_candidate(root, forged) is None, carrier_fault
    assert pp.carrier_candidate(root, landed) == candidate


def test_unwritable_status_is_a_publication_and_carrier_refusal(tmp_path):
    # Valid TOML reaches the semantic Drafted check, but the conservative writer
    # only rewrites its canonical bare-key layout. Refuse without exiting.
    root, turn, candidate, _registry, _snapshot = _first_approval_candidate(
        tmp_path, status_key='"status"'
    )
    closed, refusal = pp.publish(root, turn, candidate)
    assert closed is None and "refusing to report a flip" in refusal
    assert git(root, "rev-parse", "HEAD") == candidate
    assert git(root, "status", "--porcelain") == ""
    tree = git(root, "rev-parse", candidate + "^{tree}")
    message = "forged publication\n\n{}{}\nPublication-Candidate: {}".format(
        pp.TRAILER, turn["turn"], candidate
    )
    carrier = git(root, "commit-tree", tree, "-p", candidate, "-m", message)
    assert pp.carrier_candidate(root, carrier) is None

</file>

# WI-620 returned proof repair

This source package addresses the published [WI-619 RETURN](../contract_split/014-ADJUDICATE-3e43db9.md).
It supplies authoring and verification, not semantic acceptance. The complete
combined scope and original Context remain assigned to a fresh primary reanchor.
The coordinator remains paused; no control window, batch deletion, push or main merge.

R2: corrected the reversed attribution in
[the original packet](../wi612-authoring/OBLIGATIONS.md): IF-090 remains intake;
IF-091 remains amendment/drafted-row records and lane approval refusal. Neither
interface meaning or Status changes. The continuation explicitly names that file,
resolving the independent review's minor path ambiguity.

R3/R4: [clause-to-proof mapping](PROOFS.md) distinguishes construction checks,
actual carrier mutations, and cold read-only replay. TC-205's Method, Level and
Full tier remain unchanged; its Evidence adds the actual refusal regression.
WI-621 separately owns verification. WI-622 owns the reproduced private writer
return-contract fix. No ratchet baseline, snapshot or registry Status changes.

The initial [reproducer](status-refusal-before.txt) failed with SystemExit through
the real publication path. The helper's sole production caller already consumes
a refusal string. The fix returns that same diagnostic rather than adding catches
or expanding accepted TOML rewrite layouts. The missing-status unit test also
asserts unchanged bytes. [Focused results](refusal-and-carrier-tests.txt) follow.

This is a narrow helper repair with integration-test extensions, not a phase close.
The previous source package's stable full-suite evidence remains historical;
current focused, architecture, smoke-budget and strict checks are recorded here.
The shared test-harness initialization change also receives a fresh full-suite run.
All prior crash/process-loss, explicit residue reconciliation, fixture provenance,
Drafted-rider and separate first-approval reservations remain unchanged.

The wider focused run exposed an existing collection-order dependency in three
primary test modules: sibling imports preceded the common `load_script` setup.
The common harness now initializes the script path before collection. The
existing guarded setup in load_script remains; no per-module workaround was added. The initial collection errors and partial passing results are
preserved in `collection-before.txt`, not reported as a successful run.

The first independent source review requested the missing caller census. Its
full prompt, response and binding are retained in `round-001/`; the exact census
and every observation are resolved in [the review response](REVIEW-RESPONSE.md).

<file path="docs/reviews/wi620-authoring/PROOFS.md">
# Carrier clause-to-proof correspondence

This is an authored evidence map for fresh semantic judgment, not an acceptance
ledger. All referenced tests are real tests in the final source candidate.

| TC-205 refusal class | Directly driven carrier proof |
| --- | --- |
| Wrong WI | `test_real_first_approval_snapshot_and_successor_are_one_commit[wrong-WI]`: foreign WI/turn record and carrier name fail against the actual selected candidate. |
| Wrong turn | `test_real_return_publish_and_completion_replay`: replace the actual turn trailer with a foreign turn. |
| Stale proposal | First-approval fixture `[stale-proposal]`: replace the immutable proposal-spec blob binding in the carrier's selection. |
| Stale candidate | Return fixture: replace the candidate with the old base; also copy genuine carrier words onto a later parent. |
| Extra path | First-approval fixture `[extra-path]`: add an unreviewed file to an otherwise real publication. |
| Missing path | First-approval fixture `[missing-path]`: remove the required complete spec. The return fixture separately removes all derived effects. |
| Cross-WI close | First-approval fixture `[cross-WI-close]`: move the genuine closed spec to another WI's terminal path. |
| Malformed successor | First-approval fixture `[malformed-successor]`: replace the genuine successor with invalid spec content. |
| Partial snapshot | First-approval fixture `[partial-snapshot]`: replace only the accepted snapshot with its previous Drafted content, keeping live approval. |
| Unreviewed decision | First-approval fixture `[unreviewed-decision]`: change the completed spec's decision without changing the reviewed candidate. |

Each first-approval variant starts from a successful real publication, preserves
its parent candidate, receipt and actual adjudicator author, changes the named
surface, clears the successful-proof cache and queries the forged commit. It
asserts refusal and then successful cold recognition of the original carrier.
The turn-name variant necessarily changes the corresponding selection path and
fields together. No claim is made that every possible malformed encoding is tested.
These are representative concrete cases for every named class; the general
coverage argument is exact reconstruction, not exhaustive input enumeration.

TC-147 supplies the construction side: `test_scope_and_unjudged_absorption_refuse`,
`test_standing_snapshot_requires_complete_approved_absorption_ledger` and
`test_review_owed_candidate_requires_existing_independent_approve` check the
projection's scope, absorption and independent-review admission. The real
first-approval fixture proves byte-equal snapshot/live state, exact close,
successor and watermark in one commit. Carrier recognition then reruns that
projection and compares the complete tree. The inference that these construction
refusals propagate to recognition follows that shared call path; it is not a
claim those unit cases themselves called the carrier query.

R4: `test_real_return_publish_and_completion_replay[scoped]` and `[required-bar]`
compare every primary file byte before/after a cache-cleared carrier query. This
includes refs, index, worktree, Git objects and FETCH_HEAD, with an index-only
sentinel, unstaged changes over that entry and an untracked file. The required-bar
variant runs real regeneration and check subprocesses inside the disposable
checkout; its deterministic check output joins the exact publication tree.
The test validates the actual query's reconstruction path using small candidate
scripts, not a full product suite or an arbitrary-script sandbox guarantee.

SystemExit: inspection found `_rewrite_toml_statuses` reachable outside the
snapshot-copy exception handler. Valid quoted `status` TOML passes the semantic
Drafted predicate but is conservatively refused by the bare-key writer. The
new `test_unwritable_status_is_a_publication_and_carrier_refusal` reproduced the
process exit, then proves returned refusal for publication and the carrier query.
The helper now returns its diagnostic through its sole production caller; no
additional catch, parser expansion, cache optimization or baseline change.
Existing snapshot-copy SystemExit handling and child-process exit-code handling
remain. This resolves the demonstrated path, not every hypothetical future exit.


## Exact refusal clauses and caller census

The first-approval variants `extra-path`, `missing-path`, `cross-WI-close`,
`malformed-successor`, `partial-snapshot` and `unreviewed-decision`, plus the
return fixture's missing-effects case, are all rejected by `_verified_candidate`'s
`expected != tree` comparison. These are different contractual failure examples
through one shared exact-tree check, not separate verifier branches. In particular,
the decision/snapshot mutations change the carrier, not the candidate seen by
`approval_projection`; their construction-side coverage remains the separately
cited TC-147 tests and the explicitly labelled shared-call-path inference.
`wrong-WI` fails the immutable selected-turn binding; `stale-proposal` fails the
carrier selection's equality with the committed selection. The return fixture's
foreign turn lacks its corresponding selection record; its replaced candidate
and copied-later carrier fail the parent/candidate equality check.

The required-bar variant deliberately uses a deterministic tree-writing check as
an observation instrument. Omitting that check would omit its sentinel and fail
reconstruction. This proves the real bar invocation/replay and read-only primary
boundary using that fixture; it does not claim the production bar writes that file.

The actual [caller census](caller-census.txt), [command and source hashes](caller-census.json)
and [caller context](caller-context.txt) enumerate every literal helper reference
in all Python production source under project-trajectory/scripts. There are only
the definition and `_apply_approval`'s call, which assigns the return and immediately
propagates a nonempty refusal. The retained `_apply_flips` implementation writes
nothing and does not call this helper. The census is about this kit's production
source; it makes no claim about unknown downstream code calling a private helper.

TC-147.Evidence already names all of `tests/test_primary_publication.py`, so it
includes the new unwritable-status test's construction arm. TC-205's explicit
test reference joins its carrier-query arm. No second copy of that test is needed.

</file>

    def _rewrite_toml_statuses(live, rel, ids):
        """Move every named row's status line to `Approved` in one TOML registry.
    
        Split out of `_apply_flips` when the snapshot copy joined it (D-9 step 3):
        the carrier-specific write is a self-contained job with its own refusal, and
        keeping it inline made the caller's branch count grow every time a step was
        added to an act that is really "flip, then record what was flipped".
    
        The file's OWN newline style is preserved. The contract this writer
        advertises is that every byte except the one status cell is unchanged, and
        silently converting a CRLF registry to LF makes a one-word approval a
        whole-file diff — on exactly the registry whose diffs the amendment guard
        reads. `newline=""` keeps the bytes; the split is on the detected
        terminator."""
        # open() rather than Path.read_text: read_text only grew a `newline`
        # parameter at Python 3.13, and the kit's floor is 3.11 — on the floor the
        # keyword is a TypeError, found 2026-08-15 when the suite first ran on the
        # repo's own 3.11.9 venv (the sitting-sweep log entry carries the account).
        with open(live, "r", encoding="utf-8-sig", newline="") as fh:
            raw = fh.read()
        eol = "\r\n" if "\r\n" in raw else "\n"
        lines = raw.split(eol)
        for rid in ids:
            table = spine_carrier.REGISTRY_TABLE[rid.rsplit("-", 1)[0] + "-ID"]
            if not _flip_status_lines(lines, table, rid):
                # A located row whose status line cannot be found is a refusal to
                # write, never a silent skip: the caller already reported the flip,
                # so a no-op here would claim an approval that is not in the file.
>               raise SystemExit(
                    "intake: {} has no `{}` line under [{}.{}] — refusing to "
                    "report a flip that was not written".format(
                        live, _STATUS_KEY, table, rid
                    )

def _apply_approval(root, candidate, plan):

        refusal = intake._rewrite_toml_statuses(live, rel, list(rows))

Legacy intake entry:
def _apply_flips(root, tables, located):
    """SKIP an already-blessed row, REFUSE everything else, WRITE NOTHING; the
    sorted flipped ids, which is now always empty — and permanently so. OI-45
    (ruled 2026-08-20, executed by WI-490) settled the question this function
    used to carry open.

    **THE STATE THIS ACT MOVED FROM RETIRED AT D-9 STEP 7.** It enacted ruled
    decision 2's cheap outcome (`Modified` -> `Approved` for a row judged
    no-scope-moved); under the snapshot ladder an amendment never flips its
    row, so an amended row already reads `Approved` and there is no cell to
    move. D-9 step 7 left two candidate resolutions on the table — re-bless a
    drifted `Approved` row under loop-hold (a), or retire the arm (b) — and
    OI-45 RULED (b): the guard below is the ruled shape, not a stopgap awaiting
    an owner call.

    OI-45 IS THE RECORD, and what it retires is precise: MECHANICAL
    approval — a scripted path moving a Status cell with no judgment behind
    it. It does not say no agent may ever move a Status cell. An LLM session or
    adjudicator is fully expected to flip a row to `Approved`, and further to
    `Founded`, for spine content past the declared human-approval level
    (`agent_common.human_holds` says which) — at the human's request, or when
    working through content the level does not hold to human approval. The
    dial says who holds what; this function's refusal says only that no SCRIPT
    decides. `_cmd_snapshot`'s authority-gated refresh is the one mechanical
    door the approval record has.

    **THE MACHINERY FOR RE-BLESSING IS GONE (2026-08-20)**: the write loops it
    left standing were unreachable behind the refusal, so what looked like
    "kept intact for (a)" was dead code with a source-grep test pinning it. The
    signature keeps `root`/`tables` because the caller's contract is unchanged
    — not because a future ruling might restore a writer here; restoring one
    would be reopening OI-45, not extending this function."""
    flipped = []
    for rid, (rel, status, row, status_ix) in sorted(located.items()):
        if status is None:
            # The row exists and carries NO Status at all. Absent is not
            # "not Modified" — there is nothing here to re-verify, and skipping
            # it silently reports a clean adjudication over a row that never
            # staged one. Fail closed, naming the row.
            raise SystemExit(
                "intake: {} in {} has no `{}` — a row with no status cannot be "
                "re-verified, and treating that as a no-op would report an "
                "adjudication the registry does not record".format(
                    rid, tables[rel][0], _STATUS_KEY
                )
            )
        # RESOLVED AT D-9 STEP 7, as the guard this replaces promised. It read
        # `if status != "Modified": continue` — one silent skip over two unrelated
        # states: an already-blessed row (idempotent, correct) and a row this act
        # has no business touching (silent, wrong). The resolution splits them.
        if status == "Approved":
            # Already at the value this act writes — the one legitimate skip, and
            # the idempotence `flip_verified`'s docstring advertises.
            continue
        # Everything else is REFUSED, naming the row. Fail-CLOSED and strictly
        # less permissive than the skip it replaces:
        #   `Drafted`  a FIRST approval, not an adjudication's cheap outcome.
        #   `Founded`  ABOVE `Approved` — writing `Approved` moves it DOWN.
        #   anything   outside the closed enum, already an integrity error.
        raise SystemExit(
            'intake: {} in {} reads `{} = "{}"` — this act moves a row TO '
            "`Approved` and the only state it moved FROM (`Modified`) retired at "
            "D-9 migration step 7. Under the snapshot ladder an amendment does "
            "not flip its row at all, so there is no cell for a mechanical "
            "adjudication to move: an amended row still reads `Approved`, and "
            "what it owes is a fresh human read plus `intake.py snapshot` in the "
            "same commit. Refusing rather than skipping, so the row is named "
            "instead of being passed over.".format(
                rid, tables[rel][0], _STATUS_KEY, status
            )
        )
    # THE WRITE LOOPS AND THE COPY WENT WITH THE ARM (2026-08-20, the batch
    # review's MINOR-12). Everything that used to follow the refusal above — the
    # per-carrier status write, the CSV re-emit, and the `copy_live` that rode
    # them — was UNREACHABLE from the moment the refusal replaced the silent
    # skip: the loop now either `continue`s an already-blessed row or raises.
    # Dead code that LOOKS live is worse than no code, and a test was pinning a
    # guard on it by source grep, which reads as coverage of a path nothing can
    # execute. OI-45 RULED (b) RETIRE THE ARM (2026-08-20, executed by WI-490):
    # mechanical approval is retired for good, not deferred, and
    # `_cmd_snapshot`'s authority-gated refresh is the one mechanical door the
    # approval record has. An agent may still move a Status cell through the
    # reviewed-commit path under the declared authority dial
    # (`agent_common.human_holds`) — the retirement is of this SCRIPT, not of
    # agent judgment. Restoring writes here means reopening the ruling, not
    # un-commenting this.
    return sorted(flipped)

<file path="docs/reviews/wi620-authoring/REVIEW-RESPONSE.md">
# Response to independent source review round 1

B1: supplied the complete production caller census and source context in PROOFS.md.
Only the existing publication caller invokes the helper and it consumes the refusal.
No further production change is needed.

N1: corrected the README and review brief. The early common harness initialization
was added; the existing guarded loader setup was retained. The earlier claim that
the latter was removed was wrong. The passing full suite tested the actual bytes.

<file path="docs/reviews/wi620-authoring/round-001/review-result.txt">
**VERDICT: REQUEST_CHANGES**

**Scope read.** The `_rewrite_toml_statuses` return-contract change, its unit test, the conftest import-path move, the two new parametrized publication tests, the unwritable-status regression, the WI-620/621/622 closes, the WI-623 reanchor, the OBLIGATIONS.md correction, PROOFS.md/README.md, and the recorded check outputs. All eight recorded checks exit 0 and the pasted outputs match the metadata (3763 passed / 23 skipped full; 1696 / 4 smoke at 72.8 s under 90 s; architecture 11 passed; trace and trajectory clean with advisory warnings only). The before-fix reproducer genuinely fails with SystemExit through the real `publish` path, so the regression was red first.

## Blocking

- **B1: the "sole production caller" premise is asserted, not shown, and the failure mode if it is wrong is exactly the one the removed guard existed for.** The helper's own docstring records it was "split out of `_apply_flips`", and nothing in the package (diff, PROOFS.md, README, checks) shows a caller census. If `_apply_flips`, `flip_verified` or any other intake path still calls `_rewrite_toml_statuses` without consuming its return, this change converts a loud refusal into a silent no-op: the caller reports a flip, the file is unchanged, and (per the code's own comment) "a no-op here would claim an approval that is not in the file". A quoted-key row reaches that path the same way it reached `_apply_approval`, since the semantic reader accepts it. The green full suite does not exclude this: the only missing-status test calls the helper directly precisely because the ordinary flip path cannot be driven to that state with a bare-key fixture. Required: enumerate every caller (all of `project-trajectory/scripts/`), record the census in PROOFS.md, and if any caller ignores the return, make it consume it or return the refusal. If the census truly finds one caller, this reduces to recording it, but the WI-622 Deliverable currently claims more than the package proves.

## Nonblocking

- **N1: README/brief overstate the conftest change.** Both say the lazy `sys.path` setup "is removed from load_script", but the exact diff for `tests/conftest.py` contains only the three added lines. Either the removal is missing from the candidate or the description is wrong. Functionally harmless (a duplicate insert), but the narrative should match the bytes.
- **N2: seven of eight carrier-fault variants are discriminated by the same clause.** `extra-path`, `missing-path`, `cross-WI-close`, `malformed-successor`, `partial-snapshot`, `unreviewed-decision` and the return fixture's missing-effects case all fail at `expected != tree`; the test's own `assert forged_tree != landed_tree` makes that refusal a foregone conclusion. Only `wrong-WI` and `stale-proposal` exercise a different path (record/turn validation). PROOFS.md's "general coverage argument is exact reconstruction" is honest, but the table should say per row which clause actually refuses, so the reanchor does not read eight rows as eight independent verifier arms.
- **N3: `unreviewed-decision` and `partial-snapshot` do not drive construction-side logic through the carrier.** The candidate is unchanged, so `approval_projection` never sees a `return` classification; the forged surface simply mismatches the replay. PROOFS.md labels this as inference via the shared call path, which is the correct label; keep it that way in the reanchor.
- **N4: the required-bar fixture commits the bar's own side effect.** `check.py` writes `bar-ran`, and the post-bar `git add -A` puts it into the publication tree. That does prove the carrier replay re-ran the bar (a skipped bar would drop the file and mismatch), and the byte-exact before/after over refs, index, worktree, objects and FETCH_HEAD does settle R4. But a real bar must not write into the tree, so note in PROOFS.md that the variant proves determinism of a tree-writing bar rather than the production shape.
- **N5: hand-close shape differs from the mechanical close.** WI-620's archived spec drops the `specref` line, whereas the machinery's close writes `specref = ""` (the exact-close test pins that). Trajectory accepts both, but a hand close that differs from the published transformation is worth one line in the log.
- **N6: TC-205 Evidence placement.** `test_unwritable_status_is_a_publication_and_carrier_refusal` is primarily a construction refusal (TC-147 territory); its carrier-query assertion is the part that fits TC-205. Acceptable, but the reanchor should judge whether it belongs on one row or both.
- **N7: minor text.** Stray double space before "IF-090" in the corrected sentence (WI-620 archive and WI-623 Context). WI-623 carries four identical advisory-join blocks; noise, not error. `pytest` imported between `subprocess` and `sys` in the test module.

## Confirmed as claimed

- Refusal contract: write happens only after every id flips, so a refusal leaves bytes unchanged; the updated unit test asserts this, and the publication test asserts HEAD and a clean tree after refusal plus `carrier_candidate is None` on a forged carrier at the candidate tree.

cases); and committed output correspondence (typed verdict and specialized-session
scope/rework cases). The existing real-CLI fixture tests remain fixture tests.

<intake status writer context>
def _multiline_delims(text):
    """How many multi-line-string delimiters this line opens or closes, per
    delimiter kind — the minimum TOML awareness a line rewrite needs.

    Counted on the whole line, comment text included: a delimiter inside a `#`
    comment is not a string opener, but a `#` inside a string IS just text, and
    of the two ways to be wrong only treating a delimiter as significant is
    safe. An ODD count toggles the state; an even one — opened and closed on the
    same line, which is how a single-line triple-quoted cell reads — does not.
    """
    return {d: text.count(d) for d in _TOML_MULTILINE}


def _flip_status_lines(lines, table, rid):
    """Rewrite `[<table>.<rid>]`'s `status = ...` line to `Approved`, in place.
    True when a line moved. A LINE REWRITE ON `bootstrap.set_process_key`'s
    PATTERN, and for its reasons: stdlib has no TOML
    writer, and re-serialising the registry to change one cell would normalise
    away every comment and the file's authored ordering — a whole-file diff for
    a one-word act, on the registry whose diffs the amendment guard reads.

    IT TRACKS MULTI-LINE STRING STATE, and that is not defensive tidiness — it
    is the difference between an approval and a corruption. The spine's prose
    cells are `\"\"\"...\"\"\"` blocks that quote registry syntax freely, so a
    requirement can contain a line whose text reads `status = ...`. Rewriting by
    physical line alone edited THAT line, left the row's real `status` at
    `Modified`, and returned True — so the tool reported a flip it had not made
    while silently rewriting attested requirement text. A line inside a string
    is DATA; only a line at the table's top level is a key.

    The same state tracking is what makes the table-header scan sound: a `[` in
    the middle of a prose cell would otherwise read as the start of a new row
    and end the search early."""
    header = "[{}.{}]".format(table, rid)
    in_row = False
    open_delim = None  # the multi-line delimiter we are currently inside, if any
    for i, line in enumerate(lines):
        counts = _multiline_delims(line)
        if open_delim is not None:
            # Inside a multi-line string: this line is DATA. It can only end the
            # string, never open a table or set a key.
            if counts[open_delim] % 2:
                open_delim = None
            continue
        stripped = line.strip()
        opened = next((d for d in _TOML_MULTILINE if counts[d] % 2), None)
        if stripped.startswith("[") and stripped.endswith("]") and opened is None:
            in_row = stripped == header
            continue
        if in_row and stripped.split("=")[0].strip() == _STATUS_KEY and opened is None:
            lines[i] = '{} = "Approved"'.format(_STATUS_KEY)
            return True
        open_delim = opened
    return False


def _rewrite_toml_statuses(live, rel, ids):
    """Move named status lines to Approved; return a refusal string or None.

    Split out of `_apply_flips` when the snapshot copy joined it (D-9 step 3):
    the carrier-specific write is a self-contained job with its own refusal, and
    keeping it inline made the caller's branch count grow every time a step was
    added to an act that is really "flip, then record what was flipped".

    The file's OWN newline style is preserved. The contract this writer
    advertises is that every byte except the one status cell is unchanged, and
    silently converting a CRLF registry to LF makes a one-word approval a
    whole-file diff — on exactly the registry whose diffs the amendment guard
    reads. `newline=""` keeps the bytes; the split is on the detected
    terminator."""
    # open() rather than Path.read_text: read_text only grew a `newline`
    # parameter at Python 3.13, and the kit's floor is 3.11 — on the floor the
    # keyword is a TypeError, found 2026-08-15 when the suite first ran on the
    # repo's own 3.11.9 venv (the sitting-sweep log entry carries the account).
    with open(live, "r", encoding="utf-8-sig", newline="") as fh:
        raw = fh.read()
    eol = "\r\n" if "\r\n" in raw else "\n"
    lines = raw.split(eol)
    for rid in ids:
        table = spine_carrier.REGISTRY_TABLE[rid.rsplit("-", 1)[0] + "-ID"]
        if not _flip_status_lines(lines, table, rid):
            # A located row whose status line cannot be found is a refusal to
            # write, never a silent skip: the caller already reported the flip,
            # so a no-op here would claim an approval that is not in the file.
            return (
                "intake: {} has no `{}` line under [{}.{}] — refusing to "
                "report a flip that was not written".format(
                    live, _STATUS_KEY, table, rid
                )
            )
    live.write_text(eol.join(lines), encoding="utf-8", newline="")
